# NetaMonitor — Implementation Handbook

**Version:** 1.0  
**Prepared:** 7 November 2026  
**Status:** Product/engineering baseline for review and phased code generation  
**Project name:** NetaMonitor  
**Initial geography:** India; data model designed for later multi-country expansion

> This is a version-controlled project specification and prompt pack. Commit it to the repository as `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_HANDBOOK.md`. The repository, not any LLM conversation, is the source of truth. Each phase has three independent prompts: UI Design CodeGen, Development + Unit Testing CodeGen, and Independent E2E Testing CodeGen.

## 1. Executive delivery decision

Build an evidence-led civic accountability platform, not a one-dimensional political popularity leaderboard. The public experience must keep three concepts separate: (1) an editorial/evidence assessment of due promises, (2) verified citizens' assessments of individual promises, and (3) data/evidence coverage. A citizen vote is opinion about a promise's status; it is not proof, a government certification or an official election ballot.

The project is delivered in **27 gated phases (PH-00 to PH-26)**. Each phase has three role-separated prompts and a formal acceptance gate. The first pilot should cover one carefully selected city/district and a limited number of offices/terms with reliable sources. Do not launch nationwide with thin or unverified data.

### Selected scope included

All requested core features are included: authentication/account lifecycle; human identity and constituency verification; elected-representative directory, elections and historic terms; promises, milestones and evidence; verified citizen voting; separate evidence and citizen leaderboards; Admin CMS and approval workflow; RBAC and hidden-but-secure SuperAdmin with audit; corrections, appeals and right of reply; responsive PWA and accessibility.

All selected additional features are included: financial declarations, assets, liabilities and business interests; party and organisation affiliations, NGOs, groups, movements and protests; schemes and public works; public social accounts; side-by-side comparison; citizen submissions and moderated discussion; gamification; following and notifications; English/Hindi/Marathi; advanced offline/low-data; maps and historical ward boundaries; official-data imports; Admin analytics and anti-manipulation; transparency, methodology and funding disclosures; public API/open data; and election-period safeguards.

## 2. Product rules that must not be weakened

1. **Evidence is not sentiment.** Display source-backed editorial assessment separately from citizen evaluation. Never combine them into one opaque score.
2. **Contact confirmation is not identity verification.** Email/mobile OTP only confirms account contact. Identity and constituency eligibility are separate, reviewed states.
3. **Voting is civic feedback, not an election.** The application must never describe a promise evaluation as an official ballot, election poll or proof of delivery. Obtain legal review of election-period display and publication rules.
4. **Historical data is time-bound.** Do not overwrite party membership, office, constituency, election or financial disclosure history. Every relationship has dates and sources.
5. **Attribution matters.** Record the competent authority and the representative's actual role. An outcome controlled by another agency must not automatically be credited/debited to the politician.
6. **Sensitive publication needs review.** A sourced claim can still be disputed, incomplete or legally sensitive. Provide provenance, correction, reply and appeal workflows.
7. **Admin power is least-privilege.** SuperAdmin is a high-risk break-glass role, not an invisible bypass. Every privileged action must be logged.
8. **No paid ranking or vote manipulation.** No sponsored score, paid weight, campaign advert or partisan badge. Disclose funding.
9. **Offline is read-only for sensitive actions.** Public content may be cached under policy; voting, verification and admin work always require a live authorised server response.
10. **No platform-specific business logic.** Auth, storage, mail/push, maps, imports and hosting are adapters. PostgreSQL and documented contracts remain portable.

## 3. Architecture baseline

### 3.1 Recommended pilot stack

| Layer | Initial choice | Portability requirement |
|---|---|---|
| Web client | React + TypeScript + Vite | Standard browser app; no edge-only business rules |
| UI | Tailwind CSS + accessible component primitives + Lucide | Design tokens and component APIs stay vendor-neutral |
| Routing/data | React Router (or documented equivalent), typed API client | API contracts independent of UI framework |
| API | TypeScript + Hono on Cloudflare Workers initially | Domain/application services are runtime-neutral |
| Database | PostgreSQL, Supabase-hosted for pilot | Plain SQL migrations; avoid provider-only data model |
| Authentication | Supabase Auth initially behind `AuthProvider` | Provider-neutral identity subject and token validation |
| Object storage | Cloudflare R2 initially behind `ObjectStorageProvider` | S3-compatible/object abstraction and metadata in DB |
| Edge/static hosting | Cloudflare Pages/Workers | Container/Node-compatible deployment path documented |
| Security/abuse | Cloudflare Turnstile adapter + server rate-limits | CAPTCHA provider replaceable; never sole identity control |
| Testing | Vitest, Playwright, axe-core | CI runs from clean checkout |
| Source control/CI | GitHub + GitHub Actions | Workflows documented; avoid proprietary CI assumptions |
| Design | Figma or Penpot | Exportable assets, tokens and written specs are canonical |

**Database access rule:** the browser calls the application API for business operations. Do not expose a privileged service-role key. Server-side privileged access must be tightly scoped and every route must perform explicit authentication, permission and eligibility checks. Keep row-level security deny-by-default as defence-in-depth. Do not rely on frontend route guards for security.

### 3.2 Logical architecture

```text
Browser / Installed PWA
  ├── Public React application
  ├── Authenticated account area
  └── Admin / restricted SuperAdmin experience
          │ HTTPS + typed OpenAPI client
          ▼
Application API (Hono initially; provider-neutral)
  ├── Authentication and session validation
  ├── Authorization / RBAC / eligibility
  ├── Domain services (people, terms, promises, evidence, votes, scores)
  ├── Moderation / notifications / import orchestration
  └── Audit / telemetry / rate limiting
          │ repository interfaces
          ▼
PostgreSQL (source of truth) ─── Object storage adapter
  ├── versioned domain records      ├── public photos/media derivatives
  ├── restricted verification data  └── private evidence originals
  ├── separate ballot identity vault
  └── append-only audit events + external hash checkpoints
```

### 3.4 Context preservation / code-generator handoff

`AGENTS.md` must remain short, mandatory and current. It must identify the current accepted phase, commands, architecture constraints, security non-negotiables, documentation reading order and handoff requirements. Each phase file is the detailed execution contract. Every completed phase updates: `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, relevant domain/API/schema/security/design/test documents, the phase status and any ADR. A handoff must list changed files, migrations, env vars, commands run, test results, known defects, unresolved decisions and next-phase prerequisites. A replacement LLM must be able to continue from the repository alone.

## 4. Domain model and key records

Use a person-centred model. A person is not identical to an office, candidacy, party or term. A person may contest without winning, hold several offices over time, change parties, and represent different constituencies. Recommended domains: `Person`, `OfficeType`, `Candidacy`, `ElectionEvent`, `ElectionResult`, `ElectedTerm`, `Constituency`, `BoundaryVersion`, `PoliticalParty`, `PartyMembership`, `Promise`, `PromiseMilestone`, `PromiseSource`, `Evidence`, `Adjudication`, `Project`, `Scheme`, `ImplementingAgency`, `FinancialDisclosure`, `AssetItem`, `LiabilityItem`, `BusinessInterest`, `Organisation`, `Affiliation`, `Movement`, `ProtestEvent`, `SocialAccount`, `UserAccount`, `VerificationCase`, `VerificationEvidence`, `VoteEligibility`, `BallotToken`, `CitizenVote`, `ScoreSnapshot`, `SourceRegistry`, `CorrectionRequest`, `RightOfReply`, `Appeal`, `Submission`, `Comment`, `ModerationCase`, `FollowSubscription`, `Notification`, `AuditEvent` and `ImportJob`.

### Important relationships

- `Person 1—many ElectedTerm`; each term references one office type, constituency and election context as applicable.
- `Person 1—many Candidacy`; candidacy outcome is not assumed to be elected status.
- `Person 1—many PartyMembership`; membership is time-bounded and never overwritten.
- `Person 1—many Promise`; each promise links to originating term/campaign context, source, measurable definition, due date and responsible authority/role.
- `Promise 1—many Milestone/Evidence/Adjudication/CitizenVote`; adjudication and citizen evaluation remain separate.
- Financial disclosures are immutable dated snapshots, each with a source and declaration scope.
- Affiliation records carry a relationship type, dates, evidence and confidence; participation in an event is not equivalent to organisational membership.
- Verification evidence and vote identity mapping are restricted security domains and are never included in public profile projections.

## 5. Roles and authorization baseline

| Capability | Anonymous | Registered | Identity verified | Constituency verified | Admin (scoped) | SuperAdmin |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Browse public records | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Save/follow | No or local-only | Yes | Yes | Yes | Support only if permitted | Yes, audited |
| Submit corrections | Yes, rate-limited | Yes | Yes | Yes | Review | Review/override with reason |
| Submit evidence/comments | Limited | Yes | Yes | Yes | Moderate | Moderate |
| Vote on promise | No | No | Not unless constituency eligibility is met | Yes, eligible scope | No | No, including impersonation |
| Review identity evidence | No | No | No | No | Dedicated verification permission | Break-glass, dual control |
| Edit political records | No | No | No | No | Scoped editor permission | Yes, audited |
| Change roles | No | No | No | No | No, unless delegated role-admin permission | Yes, audited |
| Manage global settings | No | No | No | No | No | Yes, MFA + audit |

Use named permissions, not just hard-coded role names. Admin profiles: Data Editor, Verification Officer, Content Reviewer, Moderator, Support Admin and Analytics Viewer. No person may approve their own verification or content submission. Separation of duties is enforced server-side. SuperAdmin is a restricted workspace and a permission set; obscurity is not a control. Impersonation must show the true actor, reason, start/end, target account and actions. No voting, identity approval or security-role mutation while impersonating.

## 6. Verification and voting privacy design

### 6.1 Verification states

1. **Registered/contact-confirmed:** confirms control of an email/phone contact only.
2. **Identity verified:** an approved workflow has adequate basis to treat the account as a unique individual.
3. **Constituency verified:** an approved process establishes eligibility for a particular constituency. Store verification method, constituency, decision, reviewer, dates and expiry; collect the minimum evidence necessary.

Do not assume Aadhaar or voter-roll access is freely available. Do not scrape or bulk-copy electoral rolls. Do not implement biometrics or Aadhaar eKYC. Obtain Indian legal/privacy advice and provider terms before choosing any identity vendor. A manual/in-person route should be considered for accessibility and inclusion. Expire/delete temporary documents according to an approved retention schedule.

### 6.2 Ballot privacy

Use a restricted identity-to-opaque-token vault separate from vote choices. The public vote API returns aggregate distributions only. One active vote per eligible person per promise is enforced server-side with idempotency and a uniqueness rule; a controlled change-vote operation creates an auditable event while the public result reflects the latest active choice. Restrict vault access to a tiny service boundary; routine Admins cannot inspect the mapping. Public aggregates should suppress low-count cohorts (default planning threshold: 20 eligible votes, configurable after privacy review) and avoid demographic cross-tabs that re-identify small groups. Do not expose a per-user vote trail. Do not cache ballots or eligibility records offline.

### 6.3 Citizen vote options

For each promise: `fulfilled`, `partially_fulfilled`, `not_fulfilled`, `unable_to_verify`. “Unable to verify” is not counted as a positive or negative assessment. Display counts, percentages, date window, eligibility cohort and minimum-sample suppression. Explain that individual votes are confidential and the aggregate is community feedback, not an adjudicated fact.

## 7. Scoring and leaderboard specification

### 7.1 Evidence-based Promise Performance Score

For MVP, use equal commitment weights unless the public methodology board approves a later transparent alternative. Only include promises that are due, sufficiently sourced, measurable, within the representative's documented responsibility/influence, and reviewed. Let each included promise have outcome value: fulfilled = 1.0, partially fulfilled = 0.5, not fulfilled = 0.0. Then:

`Evidence Performance (%) = 100 × sum(outcome values) / number of included promises`.

Exclude not-yet-due, unverifiable, withdrawn, duplicate, out-of-scope or unattributable commitments and show the count/reasons. Never quietly drop a failed promise. Display numerator, denominator, last review date, source coverage, methodology version and evidence links. A human editor cannot type over the calculated score. Scores are recomputed deterministically from versioned decisions.

### 7.2 Citizen Assessment Index (separate from the evidence score)

For each due, eligible promise with at least the configured publication threshold (initial planning default: 20 unique eligible voters), calculate a promise-level citizen-assessed fulfilment percentage:

`Promise Citizen Assessment (%) = 100 × (fulfilled votes + 0.5 × partially-fulfilled votes) / (fulfilled + partially-fulfilled + not-fulfilled votes)`.

`unable_to_verify` votes are displayed in the distribution but excluded from this denominator. If the denominator is zero or below the threshold, display “insufficient votes” and do not publish a percentage. The representative-level **Citizen-assessed Fulfilment Index (CAFI)** is the simple, equal-weighted mean of eligible promise-level percentages. Thus a highly popular promise does not outweigh every other promise merely because it attracts more votes. Display the number of promises included, number of unique voters contributing, average/median promise-level sample size, threshold, as-of date and methodology version. Require at least three eligible promises to publish a representative-level CAFI by default; otherwise show per-promise distributions only. Keep thresholds configurable after privacy review.

Do not call CAFI a general popularity/approval rating. It represents verified participants' assessments of the fulfilment of recorded promises, not the truth of a claim, the view of all residents, or an official election poll. A representative-level roll-up must never expose small-cohort breakdowns or a user's voting history.

### 7.3 Comparison rules

Default leaderboard cohorts must be comparable: same office type and similar term/measurement window. Do not rank an MLA against a municipal councillor. Former representatives are ranked by completed term; current terms display “in progress” and only due commitments. Show “insufficient data” instead of ranking tiny samples. Party aggregates must disclose how members and term windows are attributed.

## 8. Content integrity and governance

Every material record should expose source, publisher/issuer, original date, retrieval date, record status, evidence type, editor/reviewer and correction route. Separate official/primary sources, reputable secondary research, community submissions and unverified claims. A public representative may claim a profile and reply, but cannot delete correctly sourced critical records. Corrections preserve the previous version and explain what changed. Sensitive financial/criminal/business claims require extra editorial/legal review. Record source licence/terms before importing or reproducing content/media.

## 9. India-first legal and election safeguards (legal review required)

The product may process political opinions, identity documents and constituency information. Complete a documented data-protection impact/threat assessment and obtain Indian counsel before launch. Design for the DPDP Act 2023 and DPDP Rules 2025, including their phased commencement; do not interpret this handbook as legal advice. The Government notification dated 13 November 2025 provides an 18-month commencement schedule for major provisions, which points to 13 May 2027 for those provisions. Verify current law and notifications before release.

Election-period mode must be configurable by jurisdiction, election and date, and reviewed by counsel. Consider suppressing public poll-like summaries/leaderboards during legally sensitive windows, freezing political promotion, showing an election-period notice, escalating disputed campaign content and preserving factual records. Do not assume one national rule covers every local, state and parliamentary election. Do not run paid political advertising or campaign promotion. Obtain current ECI/State Election Commission guidance and counsel's approval before enabling election-specific settings.

## 10. Non-functional quality targets

| Area | Baseline target |
|---|---|
| Accessibility | WCAG 2.2 AA target; semantic HTML; keyboard and screen-reader support |
| Responsive | Usable at 320 CSS px and common tablet/desktop widths; no horizontal overflow |
| Performance | Core Web Vitals monitored; lightweight first load; paginated/virtualised long records |
| Security | OWASP ASVS 5.0-informed verification; secrets scanning; server-side authz |
| Reliability | Health/readiness, backup/restore procedure, incident and rollback runbooks |
| Privacy | Data minimisation, role-limited access, retention/deletion, no public identity-to-vote link |
| Observability | Structured logs, metrics and traces without secrets or unnecessary PII |
| Portability | Provider adapters, SQL migrations, OpenAPI, local development and export strategy |
| PWA | Installable where supported; clear offline state; safe allowlisted cache |
| Internationalisation | English, Hindi and Marathi interface; original source text retained |

## 11. Free-tier deployment assumptions and cost warnings

The free tier is for development and a controlled pilot, not a guarantee of permanently free public infrastructure. Quotas and plans change; re-check vendor pages immediately before deployment. As of the research check for this handbook: Cloudflare Workers Free documents 100,000 requests/day and a 10 ms CPU limit; Pages Free documents up to 500 builds/month, one concurrent build and static-file limits; Cloudflare R2 documents 10 GB-month Standard storage, one million Class A operations and ten million Class B operations in its free allowance; Supabase Free lists two active projects, 500 MB database per project, 1 GB file storage and 50,000 monthly active users, and projects can pause after a week of low activity. Email delivery, reliable SMS, custom domains, identity proofing, legal review, backups/monitoring, maps/geocoding and high-volume moderation may cost money. Do not promise zero cost at national scale.

Recommended approach: Cloudflare Pages for static web; Workers for API; Supabase Postgres/Auth for pilot; R2 for object storage; Turnstile for bot mitigation; GitHub for source/CI. Monitor quotas and set budget/usage alerts. Avoid SMS OTP in MVP unless a funded provider is selected; email/contact verification is not identity proof.

## 13. Phase execution protocol

For every phase: (1) UI Design CodeGen produces and obtains design approval; (2) Development CodeGen implements approved specifications and unit/integration tests; (3) independent E2E CodeGen validates the running feature. The E2E agent does not share responsibility for writing the feature. The phase owner records acceptance. If E2E fails, create defects and repeat the necessary design/development/QA loop. Never start a dependent phase while its required acceptance gate is open.

**Phase status values:** `NOT_STARTED`, `IN_DESIGN`, `DESIGN_APPROVED`, `IN_DEVELOPMENT`, `DEV_COMPLETE`, `IN_E2E`, `BLOCKED`, `ACCEPTED`. Each phase file must include owner, start/end dates, dependency acceptance IDs, PR links, deployment environment, test run IDs, known risks and sign-off.

**Standard phase deliverables:** UI inventory and designs; approved domain/API contracts; migrations; application code; unit/integration results; independent E2E report; accessibility/security findings; updated docs; release notes; rollback notes; handoff.

## 15. Complete phase specifications and three independent prompts per phase

Each prompt below is standalone: copy the full prompt block for the assigned agent. The shared project context and constraints are repeated inside each block so that a prompt can be used in a new code-generator session without relying on conversation memory. Agents must still read the repository documents.

---

# PH-00 — Product definition, governance and decision baseline

**Dependencies:** None  
**Goal:** Freeze the product contract before software implementation: scope, user journeys, trust model, data policy, score definitions, MVP boundaries and decision records.

### Phase specification

**UI/design scope:** Produce a service blueprint, personas, user journeys, information architecture, low-fidelity flow maps and responsive interaction principles. Do not create polished screens yet. Cover anonymous browsing, verified voting, corrections, right of reply and admin review.

**Development scope:** Create the product specification pack only: requirements catalogue, glossary, domain boundaries, initial ERD, state machines, score specification, permissions matrix, privacy threat model, data-source register template, ADR process and phase acceptance checklist. Do not scaffold the runtime app yet.

**Independent E2E scope:** Create a test strategy and executable acceptance-test catalogue from the agreed requirements. Define user roles, synthetic test data, risk-based scenarios, traceability from requirement IDs to tests, and test environments. Do not test or approve implementation that does not exist.

**Acceptance gate:** Approved PRD and non-functional requirements; approved domain glossary and data model v1; score methodology v1; privacy, safety, moderation and election-period policy drafts; documented open decisions; traceability matrix; no unresolved critical architecture decision.

### Prompt A — UI Design CodeGen

```text
You are an independent specialist code-generation agent working on NetaMonitor, an India-first, non-partisan public-information and civic-accountability web application. Its purpose is to let people inspect elected representatives, time-bounded offices/terms/party history, sourced promises and evidence, public projects and disclosures, then submit confidential verified-citizen evaluations. It is not an official election system, a political campaign tool, a popularity contest or a substitute for legal/electoral processes. The selected scope includes every feature in the phase roadmap: authentication; human and constituency verification; people/elections/terms; promises/evidence; voting; separate evidence and citizen measures; Admin and restricted SuperAdmin; corrections/replies/appeals; financial/business records; affiliations/NGOs/movements/protests/social accounts; schemes/projects; comparison; submissions/comments; gamification; notifications; English/Hindi/Marathi; maps/historical boundaries; imports; analytics/anti-manipulation; transparency/OpenAPI/open data; election-period safeguards; responsive PWA/offline mode.

FIRST ACTION: inspect the repository and read `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/PRODUCT_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `docs/SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md`, `docs/SECURITY_MODEL.md`, `docs/DATA_DICTIONARY.md`, `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`, `docs/RBAC_MATRIX.md`, `docs/SCORING_METHODOLOGY.md`, `docs/VERIFICATION_POLICY.md`, `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `docs/MODERATION_POLICY.md`, the relevant ADRs and this phase file. If a file does not yet exist, create or update it as required by the phase; do not invent undocumented decisions. Repository documentation is the source of truth, never a prior chat transcript. Do not ask the user to repeat requirements already recorded. If a requirement conflicts with an approved ADR or security policy, stop that specific change, explain the conflict in the handoff and propose an ADR rather than silently changing architecture.

PORTABILITY: use TypeScript, a React/Vite frontend, an API boundary (Hono/TypeScript is the initial adapter), PostgreSQL as the relational source of truth, SQL migrations, OpenAPI contracts and provider interfaces. Initial hosting may use Cloudflare Pages/Workers, Supabase PostgreSQL/Auth and Cloudflare R2, but domain logic must not depend on those vendors. Keep auth, object storage, email/push, maps/geocoding and external data sources behind replaceable adapters. No client-side secrets. Do not place privileged service-role keys in browser code. All authorisation and business rules are enforced server-side. Apply deny-by-default database/API permissions and least privilege.

TRUST AND PRIVACY NON-NEGOTIABLES: distinguish registered/contact-confirmed, identity-verified and constituency-verified. OTP is not proof of identity or residence. Do not scrape voter rolls or implement Aadhaar biometric/eKYC. Minimise identity data; encrypt temporary verification evidence; apply access limits and retention/deletion. Separate voter identity mapping from ballot choice; public APIs expose aggregates only and suppress small cohorts. No public individual vote history. Admins must not approve themselves. SuperAdmin is hidden from ordinary navigation but is not secured by obscurity; enforce server-side permissions, MFA, visible support impersonation and audit it. Impersonation cannot cast votes or make identity/verification decisions. Audit logs are append-only and tamper-evident with hash chaining and independent checkpoints; do not promise that a single database is absolutely immutable.

CONTENT AND SCORING: every material factual assertion needs source, attribution, date, status and correction path. Separate sourced/editorial assessment from citizen vote distributions. Do not turn association into proof of wrongdoing. Financial amounts must be dated, sourced and labelled self-declared/officially recorded/corroborated; never call them independently audited net worth without proof. A promise score includes only due, assessable, attributable commitments; default equal weighting and a public versioned formula; exclude not-due, unverifiable, withdrawn and out-of-scope commitments with reasons. Citizen measures remain separate and display sample sizes; do not treat votes as proof or official election results. No paid ranking, no paid vote weighting, no political advertising in the MVP.

ENGINEERING QUALITY: use semantic accessible HTML, keyboard support, WCAG 2.2 AA target, responsive mobile-first layouts, safe validation, output encoding, rate limiting, structured logs without secrets/PII, migrations and rollback, unit/integration tests, and deterministic synthetic fixtures. Use OWASP ASVS 5.0 as a security verification reference. Do not use real people's identity documents or real voter records in tests. All test identities and politicians must be clearly synthetic. Never commit credentials, personal data, generated production data or local secrets. Never weaken security to make a test pass.

DELIVERY DISCIPLINE: work only on the assigned phase and its accepted dependencies. Keep changes reviewable. Do not implement unrelated future phases. Update `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, the current phase file, architecture/data/API/security documents and ADRs whenever they change. Include exact file paths, migrations, env vars, tests and commands in the handoff. Do not claim completion unless every acceptance criterion is demonstrated. Do not deploy to production or incur paid services without explicit approval. Use free-tier assumptions only for a pilot and instrument usage/quotas.

PHASE ASSIGNMENT: PH-00 — Product definition, governance and decision baseline
ROLE: You are Agent 1: UI/UX DESIGN CODEGEN. You own experience design and design specifications, not production business logic. Deliver user flows, screen inventory, responsive layouts for small mobile/tablet/desktop, interaction states, component specifications, design tokens, content hierarchy, accessibility annotations, empty/loading/error/offline states, and Figma/Penpot handoff references where applicable. Use existing design system and do not invent backend data or permissions. For each screen, map actions to API capabilities and note loading, validation, permission and failure states. Provide annotated acceptance criteria and design QA checklist. If this phase has no end-user UI, create the relevant diagrams/specifications and explicitly state that no UI is required. Do not write application code except optional throwaway design prototypes clearly marked as such.

PHASE GOAL: Freeze the product contract before software implementation: scope, user journeys, trust model, data policy, score definitions, MVP boundaries and decision records.

YOUR PHASE-SPECIFIC TASK: Produce a service blueprint, personas, user journeys, information architecture, low-fidelity flow maps and responsive interaction principles. Do not create polished screens yet. Cover anonymous browsing, verified voting, corrections, right of reply and admin review.

DESIGN ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: Approved PRD and non-functional requirements; approved domain glossary and data model v1; score methodology v1; privacy, safety, moderation and election-period policy drafts; documented open decisions; traceability matrix; no unresolved critical architecture decision.

DELIVERABLES: Produce/update `docs/phases/PH-00/phase-summary.md`, relevant screen/flow/component specifications, design tokens or design references, responsive annotations, interaction/error/loading/permission states, accessibility notes, and design handoff. Mark each requirement with a stable ID (for example `PH-00-UI-001`) so Development and E2E can trace it. Include a concise “Design Decisions” section and unresolved questions. Do not modify unrelated phases. Obtain design approval before implementation begins.
```

---

# PH-01 — Repository, architecture and delivery foundation

**Dependencies:** 00  
**Goal:** Create a portable monorepo, local development environment, CI checks, architecture boundaries, API conventions, migrations and durable project memory.

### Phase specification

**UI/design scope:** Define design handoff conventions, screen specification format, Figma/Penpot file organisation, design-token naming, responsive annotation standards and UI-to-requirement traceability. Create a neutral app-shell blueprint and no production feature screens.

**Development scope:** Create the monorepo and working skeleton using React + TypeScript + Vite, Hono/TypeScript API, PostgreSQL migrations, shared packages, lint/format/typecheck/test scripts, OpenAPI baseline, local environment, CI and deployment adapters. Implement health/readiness endpoints and a minimal accessible shell only.

**Independent E2E scope:** Create the independent test harness, browser matrix, test data lifecycle, CI invocation, smoke test for the shell and API health endpoint, artifact/report convention and failure triage protocol. Verify clean checkout can install, build and run using documented steps.

**Acceptance gate:** Fresh clone setup works; CI passes; no secrets committed; package boundaries and ADRs documented; local database/migrations reproducible; test reports published; deployment provider can be changed without rewriting domain logic.

Audit logs are append-only and tamper-evident with hash chaining and independent checkpoints; do not promise that a single database is absolutely immutable.

CONTENT AND SCORING: every material factual assertion needs source, attribution, date, status and correction path. Separate sourced/editorial assessment from citizen vote distributions. Do not turn association into proof of wrongdoing. Financial amounts must be dated, sourced and labelled self-declared/officially recorded/corroborated; never call them independently audited net worth without proof. A promise score includes only due, assessable, attributable commitments; default equal weighting and a public versioned formula; exclude not-due, unverifiable, withdrawn and out-of-scope commitments with reasons. Citizen measures remain separate and display sample sizes; do not treat votes as proof or official election results. No paid ranking, no paid vote weighting, no political advertising in the MVP.

ENGINEERING QUALITY: use semantic accessible HTML, keyboard support, WCAG 2.2 AA target, responsive mobile-first layouts, safe validation, output encoding, rate limiting, structured logs without secrets/PII, migrations and rollback, unit/integration tests, and deterministic synthetic fixtures. Use OWASP ASVS 5.0 as a security verification reference. Do not use real people's identity documents or real voter records in tests. All test identities and politicians must be clearly synthetic. Never commit credentials, personal data, generated production data or local secrets. Never weaken security to make a test pass.

DELIVERY DISCIPLINE: work only on the assigned phase and its accepted dependencies. Keep changes reviewable. Do not implement unrelated future phases. Update `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, the current phase file, architecture/data/API/security documents and ADRs whenever they change. Include exact file paths, migrations, env vars, tests and commands in the handoff. Do not claim completion unless every acceptance criterion is demonstrated. Do not deploy to production or incur paid services without explicit approval. Use free-tier assumptions only for a pilot and instrument usage/quotas.

PHASE ASSIGNMENT: PH-01 — Repository, architecture and delivery foundation
ROLE: You are Agent 2: DEVELOPMENT + UNIT/INTEGRATION TEST CODEGEN. You own implementation for this phase. Implement only approved designs and contracts. Before coding, list dependencies, impacted packages, schema/API changes and risks. Add forward and rollback migrations, validation, permission checks, telemetry without sensitive data, unit and integration tests, deterministic fixtures and documentation updates. Keep code modular and provider-independent. Do not modify requirements to fit a convenient implementation. If design/API contracts are incomplete, write an explicit question/blocker rather than inventing behaviour. Run formatting, lint, typecheck, unit/integration tests and build; record exact outcomes. Do not write or approve the independent E2E suite.

PHASE GOAL: Create a portable monorepo, local development environment, CI checks, architecture boundaries, API conventions, migrations and durable project memory.

YOUR PHASE-SPECIFIC TASK: Create the monorepo and working skeleton using React + TypeScript + Vite, Hono/TypeScript API, PostgreSQL migrations, shared packages, lint/format/typecheck/test scripts, OpenAPI baseline, local environment, CI and deployment adapters. Implement health/readiness endpoints and a minimal accessible shell only.

DEPENDENCIES: 00. Verify that each dependency is marked ACCEPTED in the repository. If a dependency is not accepted, do not bypass it; implement only a separately approved non-dependent preparatory task and record the blocker.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: Fresh clone setup works; CI passes; no secrets committed; package boundaries and ADRs documented; local database/migrations reproducible; test reports published; deployment provider can be changed without rewriting domain logic.

REQUIRED OUTPUT: working code; documented API and data changes; forward/rollback migrations where applicable; environment-variable template updates (never actual secrets); unit and integration tests; accessibility/security checks relevant to the phase; exact command/test results; updated `docs/phases/PH-01/phase-summary.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `AGENTS.md` and impacted architecture/domain/API/security/test docs. Provide a handoff with changed files, migration IDs, env vars, test commands/results, residual defects, operational notes and next-phase readiness. Do not mark ACCEPTED; E2E and phase-owner sign-off are required.
```

PHASE ASSIGNMENT: PH-01 — Repository, architecture and delivery foundation
ROLE: You are Agent 3: INDEPENDENT E2E / QA CODEGEN. You are independent of the developer and must test the running feature against requirements, not merely inspect unit tests. Read the approved designs and acceptance criteria; create/use synthetic fixtures and separate test accounts for each role. Exercise real browser flows and API boundaries, permissions, validation, failure states, responsive viewports and privacy/security edge cases relevant to this phase. Do not change production code to hide defects. You may create test-only utilities/fixtures and isolated test configuration. Execute tests and save reports, screenshots/video/traces where useful, reproducible defect tickets with severity, steps, expected/actual results and evidence. Distinguish a blocked test from a passed test. Do not mark the phase accepted while critical/high defects or untested acceptance criteria remain. Do not access production personal data.

PHASE GOAL: Create a portable monorepo, local development environment, CI checks, architecture boundaries, API conventions, migrations and durable project memory.

YOUR PHASE-SPECIFIC TEST MISSION: Create the independent test harness, browser matrix, test data lifecycle, CI invocation, smoke test for the shell and API health endpoint, artifact/report convention and failure triage protocol. Verify clean checkout can install, build and run using documented steps.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA UNDER TEST: Fresh clone setup works; CI passes; no secrets committed; package boundaries and ADRs documented; local database/migrations reproducible; test reports published; deployment provider can be changed without rewriting domain logic.

TEST EXECUTION: Read the design and developer handoff, then create a requirement-to-test traceability table. Use synthetic fixtures only and a clean test environment. Test all changed browser flows and API permission boundaries, relevant device sizes, error/empty/loading conditions, accessibility and privacy/security cases. Run the tests independently; do not infer a pass from developer reports. Save Playwright tests, test data setup/teardown, screenshots/traces where relevant, machine-readable result and human-readable report under `tests/e2e/PH-01/` and `docs/phases/PH-01/phase-summary.md`.

EXIT REPORT: Return PASS / FAIL / BLOCKED for each acceptance criterion, total pass/fail/blocked counts, exact commands, environment/build ID, defect severity and reproducible steps. Any critical/high defect, privacy leak, privilege bypass, inaccurate score, vote integrity failure or missing test blocks acceptance. Do not alter application code to hide a defect. Recommend ACCEPTED only when every required criterion is tested and passed.

---

# PH-02 — Design system, responsive shell and initial PWA shell

**Dependencies:** 00, 01  
**Goal:** Establish the visual language, accessible component library, navigation shell and installable PWA foundation.

### Phase specification

**UI/design scope:** Create polished responsive designs for desktop, tablet and mobile: design tokens, typography, colour themes, spacing, cards, tables, forms, navigation, skeletons, empty/error/offline states, keyboard focus, reduced motion and app install flow. Cover light and dark themes.

**Development scope:** Implement design tokens and reusable accessible UI primitives, responsive app shell, routes/placeholders, theme handling, icons, typography, manifest, app icons, service-worker registration and safe static-asset caching. Do not cache authenticated or sensitive API responses.

**Independent E2E scope:** Validate design-system components and shell at phone/tablet/desktop viewports, keyboard-only, screen-reader landmarks, colour contrast, reduced motion, theme persistence and PWA manifest/service-worker basics. Add screenshot baselines and visual regression checks.

**Acceptance gate:** Approved responsive designs; components documented and reusable; no horizontal overflow at supported widths; keyboard/focus states pass; PWA is installable where supported; offline fallback exists; no private data is cached.

Use OWASP ASVS 5.0 as a security verification reference. Do not use real people's identity documents or real voter records in tests. All test identities and politicians must be clearly synthetic. Never commit credentials, personal data, generated production data or local secrets. Never weaken security to make a test pass.

DELIVERY DISCIPLINE: work only on the assigned phase and its accepted dependencies. Keep changes reviewable. Do not implement unrelated future phases. Update `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, the current phase file, architecture/data/API/security documents and ADRs whenever they change. Include exact file paths, migrations, env vars, tests and commands in the handoff. Do not claim completion unless every acceptance criterion is demonstrated. Do not deploy to production or incur paid services without explicit approval. Use free-tier assumptions only for a pilot and instrument usage/quotas.

PHASE ASSIGNMENT: PH-02 — Design system, responsive shell and initial PWA shell
ROLE: You are Agent 3: INDEPENDENT E2E / QA CODEGEN. You are independent of the developer and must test the running feature against requirements, not merely inspect unit tests. Read the approved designs and acceptance criteria; create/use synthetic fixtures and separate test accounts for each role. Exercise real browser flows and API boundaries, permissions, validation, failure states, responsive viewports and privacy/security edge cases relevant to this phase. Do not change production code to hide defects. You may create test-only utilities/fixtures and isolated test configuration. Execute tests and save reports, screenshots/video/traces where useful, reproducible defect tickets with severity, steps, expected/actual results and evidence. Distinguish a blocked test from a passed test. Do not mark the phase accepted while critical/high defects or untested acceptance criteria remain. Do not access production personal data.

PHASE GOAL: Establish the visual language, accessible component library, navigation shell and installable PWA foundation.

YOUR PHASE-SPECIFIC TEST MISSION: Validate design-system components and shell at phone/tablet/desktop viewports, keyboard-only, screen-reader landmarks, colour contrast, reduced motion, theme persistence and PWA manifest/service-worker basics. Add screenshot baselines and visual regression checks.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA UNDER TEST: Approved responsive designs; components documented and reusable; no horizontal overflow at supported widths; keyboard/focus states pass; PWA is installable where supported; offline fallback exists; no private data is cached.

TEST EXECUTION: Read the design and developer handoff, then create a requirement-to-test traceability table. Use synthetic fixtures only and a clean test environment. Test all changed browser flows and API permission boundaries, relevant device sizes, error/empty/loading conditions, accessibility and privacy/security cases. Run the tests independently; do not infer a pass from developer reports. Save Playwright tests, test data setup/teardown, screenshots/traces where relevant, machine-readable result and human-readable report under `tests/e2e/PH-02/` and `docs/phases/PH-02/phase-summary.md`.

EXIT REPORT: Return PASS / FAIL / BLOCKED for each acceptance criterion, total pass/fail/blocked counts, exact commands, environment/build ID, defect severity and reproducible steps. Any critical/high defect, privacy leak, privilege bypass, inaccurate score, vote integrity failure or missing test blocks acceptance. Do not alter application code to hide a defect. Recommend ACCEPTED only when every required criterion is tested and passed.
```

# PH-03 — Registration, authentication and account lifecycle

**Dependencies:** 01, 02  
**Goal:** Implement secure registration, sign-in, session management and account lifecycle using replaceable authentication adapters.

### Phase specification

**UI/design scope:** Design registration, login, email/phone confirmation, password reset, session-expiry, account settings, privacy notice acknowledgement, consent history, deletion request and accessible error states. Explain clearly that contact verification is not identity verification.

**Development scope:** Integrate the selected auth provider behind an AuthProvider interface (initial adapter may use Supabase Auth). Implement API token verification, account profile creation, session refresh/logout, email verification, recovery, rate limits, CAPTCHA/Turnstile adapter, consent records and safe account deletion workflow. Keep secrets server-side.

**Independent E2E scope:** Test registration, duplicate identities, invalid/expired tokens, confirmation, login/logout, refresh, reset, session expiry, brute-force/rate limits, CAPTCHA failure, CSRF/CORS and account deletion/export flows. Confirm a contact-verified user remains unable to vote.

**Acceptance gate:** Auth flows work end to end; sessions are validated server-side; no client secret exposure; consent and privacy actions are auditable; contact verification does not grant voting eligibility; provider adapter is replaceable.

### Prompt A — UI Design CodeGen

```text
You are an independent specialist code-generation agent working on NetaMonitor, an India-first, non-partisan public-information and civic-accountability web application. Its purpose is to let people inspect elected representatives, time-bounded offices/terms/party history, sourced promises and evidence, public projects and disclosures, then submit confidential verified-citizen evaluations. It is not an official election system, a political campaign tool, a popularity contest or a substitute for legal/electoral processes. The selected scope includes every feature in the phase roadmap: authentication; human and constituency verification; people/elections/terms; promises/evidence; voting; separate evidence and citizen measures; Admin and restricted SuperAdmin; corrections/replies/appeals; financial/business records; affiliations/NGOs/movements/protests/social accounts; schemes/projects; comparison; submissions/comments; gamification; notifications; English/Hindi/Marathi; maps/historical boundaries; imports; analytics/anti-manipulation; transparency/OpenAPI/open data; election-period safeguards; responsive PWA/offline mode.

FIRST ACTION: inspect the repository and read `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/PRODUCT_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `docs/SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md`, `docs/SECURITY_MODEL.md`, `docs/DATA_DICTIONARY.md`, `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`, `docs/RBAC_MATRIX.md`, `docs/SCORING_METHODOLOGY.md`, `docs/VERIFICATION_POLICY.md`, `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `docs/MODERATION_POLICY.md`, the relevant ADRs and this phase file. If a file does not yet exist, create or update it as required by the phase; do not invent undocumented decisions. Repository documentation is the source of truth, never a prior chat transcript. Do not ask the user to repeat requirements already recorded. If a requirement conflicts with an approved ADR or security policy, stop that specific change, explain the conflict in the handoff and propose an ADR rather than silently changing architecture.

PORTABILITY: use TypeScript, a React/Vite frontend, an API boundary (Hono/TypeScript is the initial adapter), PostgreSQL as the relational source of truth, SQL migrations, OpenAPI contracts and provider interfaces. Initial hosting may use Cloudflare Pages/Workers, Supabase PostgreSQL/Auth and Cloudflare R2, but domain logic must not depend on those vendors. Keep auth, object storage, email/push, maps/geocoding and external data sources behind replaceable adapters. No client-side secrets. Do not place privileged service-role keys in browser code. All authorisation and business rules are enforced server-side. Apply deny-by-default database/API permissions and least privilege.

TRUST AND PRIVACY NON-NEGOTIABLES: distinguish registered/contact-confirmed, identity-verified and constituency-verified. OTP is not proof of identity or residence. Do not scrape voter rolls or implement Aadhaar biometric/eKYC. Minimise identity data; encrypt temporary verification evidence; apply access limits and retention/deletion. Separate voter identity mapping from ballot choice; public APIs expose aggregates only and suppress small cohorts. No public individual vote history. Admins must not approve themselves. SuperAdmin is hidden from ordinary navigation but is not secured by obscurity; enforce server-side permissions, MFA, visible support impersonation and audit it. Impersonation cannot cast votes or make identity/verification decisions. Audit logs are append-only and tamper-evident with hash chaining and independent checkpoints; do not promise that a single database is absolutely immutable.

CONTENT AND SCORING: every material factual assertion needs source, attribution, date, status and correction path. Separate sourced/editorial assessment from citizen vote distributions. Do not turn association into proof of wrongdoing. Financial amounts must be dated, sourced and labelled self-declared/officially recorded/corroborated; never call them independently audited net worth without proof. A promise score includes only due, assessable, attributable commitments; default equal weighting and a public versioned formula; exclude not-due, unverifiable, withdrawn and out-of-scope commitments with reasons. Citizen measures remain separate and display sample sizes; do not treat votes as proof or official election results. No paid ranking, no paid vote weighting, no political advertising in the MVP.

ENGINEERING QUALITY: use semantic accessible HTML, keyboard support, WCAG 2.2 AA target, responsive mobile-first layouts, safe validation, output encoding, rate limiting, structured logs without secrets/PII, migrations and rollback, unit/integration tests, and deterministic synthetic fixtures. Use OWASP ASVS 5.0 as a security verification reference. Do not use real people's identity documents or real voter records in tests. All test identities and politicians must be clearly synthetic. Never commit credentials, personal data, generated production data or local secrets. Never weaken security to make a test pass.

DELIVERY DISCIPLINE: work only on the assigned phase and its accepted dependencies. Keep changes reviewable. Do not implement unrelated future phases. Update `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, the current phase file, architecture/data/API/security documents and ADRs whenever they change. Include exact file paths, migrations, env vars, tests and commands in the handoff. Do not claim completion unless every acceptance criterion is demonstrated. Do not deploy to production or incur paid services without explicit approval. Use free-tier assumptions only for a pilot and instrument usage/quotas.

PHASE ASSIGNMENT: PH-03 — Registration, authentication and account lifecycle
ROLE: You are Agent 1: UI/UX DESIGN CODEGEN. You own experience design and design specifications, not production business logic. Deliver user flows, screen inventory, responsive layouts for small mobile/tablet/desktop, interaction states, component specifications, design tokens, content hierarchy, accessibility annotations, empty/loading/error/offline states, and Figma/Penpot handoff references where applicable. Use existing design system and do not invent backend data or permissions. For each screen, map actions to API capabilities and note loading, validation, permission and failure states. Provide annotated acceptance criteria and design QA checklist. If this phase has no end-user UI, create the relevant diagrams/specifications and explicitly state that no UI is required. YOUR PHASE-SPECIFIC TASK: Design registration, login, email/phone confirmation, password reset, session-expiry, account settings, privacy notice acknowledgement, consent history, deletion request and accessible error states. Explain clearly that contact verification is not identity verification.

DESIGN ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: Auth flows work end to end; sessions are validated server-side; no client secret exposure; consent and privacy actions are auditable; contact verification does not grant voting eligibility; provider adapter is replaceable.

DELIVERABLES: Produce/update `docs/phases/PH-03/phase-summary.md`, relevant screen/flow/component specifications, design tokens or design references, responsive annotations, interaction/error/loading/permission states, accessibility notes, and design handoff. Mark each requirement with a stable ID (for example `PH-03-UI-001`) so Development and E2E can trace it. Include a concise “Design Decisions” section and unresolved questions. Do not modify unrelated phases. Obtain design approval before implementation begins.
```

# PH-04 — RBAC, Admin roles, SuperAdmin and tamper-evident audit

**Dependencies:** 01, 03  
**Goal:** Enforce least-privilege permissions, separate Admin workspaces, privileged SuperAdmin controls, guarded support impersonation and auditability.

### Phase specification

**UI/design scope:** Design Admin navigation by permission, access-denied states, role assignment, privileged-action confirmation, audit viewer and a restricted SuperAdmin workspace. Keep SuperAdmin absent from ordinary user navigation; do not rely on URL secrecy. Show impersonation banners and exit controls.

**Development scope:** Implement server-side RBAC and permission checks, role/permission tables, Admin permission profiles, SuperAdmin bootstrap procedure, MFA requirement, break-glass workflow, session actor/subject fields, guarded impersonation, append-only audit events, hash chaining and external audit-checkpoint export adapter. Block voting and verification decisions during impersonation.

**Independent E2E scope:** Attempt horizontal/vertical privilege escalation on every API, direct URL access, role changes, expired privileged sessions, MFA bypass, unauthorized audit mutation, impersonation abuse and vote attempts while impersonating. Verify hash-chain tamper detection and audit entries for sensitive actions.

**Acceptance gate:** All sensitive endpoints enforce server permissions; no hidden-URL-only security; Admin duties are separable; SuperAdmin requires MFA; impersonation is conspicuous and logged; impersonated sessions cannot vote; audit history is append-only and tamper-evident, with external checkpoints.

Financial amounts must be dated, sourced and labelled self-declared/officially recorded/corroborated; never call them independently audited net worth without proof. A promise score includes only due, assessable, attributable commitments; default equal weighting and a public versioned formula; exclude not-due, unverifiable, withdrawn and out-of-scope commitments with reasons. Citizen measures remain separate and display sample sizes; do not treat votes as proof or official election results. No paid ranking, no paid vote weighting, no political advertising in the MVP.

ENGINEERING QUALITY: use semantic accessible HTML, keyboard support, WCAG 2.2 AA target, responsive mobile-first layouts, safe validation, output encoding, rate limiting, structured logs without secrets/PII, migrations and rollback, unit/integration tests, and deterministic synthetic fixtures. Use OWASP ASVS 5.0 as a security verification reference. Do not use real people's identity documents or real voter records in tests. All test identities and politicians must be clearly synthetic. Never commit credentials, personal data, generated production data or local secrets. Never weaken security to make a test pass.

DELIVERY DISCIPLINE: work only on the assigned phase and its accepted dependencies. Keep changes reviewable. Do not implement unrelated future phases. Update `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, the current phase file, architecture/data/API/security documents and ADRs whenever they change. Include exact file paths, migrations, env vars, tests and commands in the handoff. Do not claim completion unless every acceptance criterion is demonstrated. Do not deploy to production or incur paid services without explicit approval. Use free-tier assumptions only for a pilot and instrument usage/quotas.

PHASE ASSIGNMENT: PH-04 — RBAC, Admin roles, SuperAdmin and tamper-evident audit
ROLE: You are Agent 1: UI/UX DESIGN CODEGEN. You own experience design and design specifications, not production business logic. Deliver user flows, screen inventory, responsive layouts for small mobile/tablet/desktop, interaction states, component specifications, design tokens, content hierarchy, accessibility annotations, empty/loading/error/offline states, and Figma/Penpot handoff references where applicable. Use existing design system and do not invent backend data or permissions. For each screen, map actions to API capabilities and note loading, validation, permission and failure states. Provide annotated acceptance criteria and design QA checklist. If this phase has no end-user UI, create the relevant diagrams/specifications and explicitly state that no UI is required. Do not write application code except optional throwaway design prototypes clearly marked as such.

PHASE GOAL: Enforce least-privilege permissions, separate Admin workspaces, privileged SuperAdmin controls, guarded support impersonation and auditability.

YOUR PHASE-SPECIFIC TASK: Design Admin navigation by permission, access-denied states, role assignment, privileged-action confirmation, audit viewer and a restricted SuperAdmin workspace. Keep SuperAdmin absent from ordinary user navigation; do not rely on URL secrecy. Show impersonation banners and exit controls.

DESIGN ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: All sensitive endpoints enforce server permissions; no hidden-URL-only security; Admin duties are separable; SuperAdmin requires MFA; impersonation is conspicuous and logged; impersonated sessions cannot vote; audit history is append-only and tamper-evident, with external checkpoints.

DELIVERABLES: Produce/update `docs/phases/PH-04/phase-summary.md`, relevant screen/flow/component specifications, design tokens or design references, responsive annotations, interaction/error/loading/permission states, accessibility notes, and design handoff. Mark each requirement with a stable ID (for example `PH-04-UI-001`) so Development and E2E can trace it. Include a concise “Design Decisions” section and unresolved questions. Do not modify unrelated phases. Obtain design approval before implementation begins.

---

# PH-05 — Identity and constituency verification workflow

**Dependencies:** 03, 04, 06  
**Goal:** Establish separate contact, identity and constituency-eligibility states while minimising and protecting personal data.

### Phase specification

**UI/design scope:** Design a progressive verification wizard, accessible alternative routes, document upload guidance, manual review queue, request-for-more-information, rejection reason, expiry/reverification and appeal screens. Explain what is collected, why, who can see it and retention.

**Development scope:** Implement verification application states, evidence metadata, encrypted temporary document storage, restricted reviewer access, reviewer separation-of-duties, constituency eligibility evidence, decision reason codes, retention/deletion jobs, appeals, audit trails and provider/manual verification adapters. Do not implement Aadhaar biometric/eKYC or scrape electoral rolls.

**Independent E2E scope:** Test the three distinct states (registered, identity-verified, constituency-verified), reviewer permissions, self-approval prevention, document leakage, signed URL expiry, upload validation, rejection/appeal, retention deletion and inability to vote outside eligibility. Test non-digital/manual exception route.

**Acceptance gate:** Minimum necessary personal data only; no identity document public/API leakage; reviewer actions audited; no self-approval; verification does not imply citizenship/residency beyond evidence; vote eligibility checks require the correct verification tier and constituency.

Include exact file paths, migrations, env vars, tests and commands in the handoff. Do not claim completion unless every acceptance criterion is demonstrated. Do not deploy to production or incur paid services without explicit approval. Use free-tier assumptions only for a pilot and instrument usage/quotas.

PHASE ASSIGNMENT: PH-05 — Identity and constituency verification workflow
ROLE: You are Agent 2: DEVELOPMENT + UNIT/INTEGRATION TEST CODEGEN. You own implementation for this phase. Implement only approved designs and contracts. Before coding, list dependencies, impacted packages, schema/API changes and risks. Add forward and rollback migrations, validation, permission checks, telemetry without sensitive data, unit and integration tests, deterministic fixtures and documentation updates. Keep code modular and provider-independent. Do not modify requirements to fit a convenient implementation. If design/API contracts are incomplete, write an explicit question/blocker rather than inventing behaviour. Run formatting, lint, typecheck, unit/integration tests and build; record exact outcomes. Do not write or approve the independent E2E suite.

PHASE GOAL: Establish separate contact, identity and constituency-eligibility states while minimising and protecting personal data.

YOUR PHASE-SPECIFIC TASK: Implement verification application states, evidence metadata, encrypted temporary document storage, restricted reviewer access, reviewer separation-of-duties, constituency eligibility evidence, decision reason codes, retention/deletion jobs, appeals, audit trails and provider/manual verification adapters. Do not implement Aadhaar biometric/eKYC or scrape electoral rolls.

DEPENDENCIES: 03, 04, 06. Verify that each dependency is marked ACCEPTED in the repository. If a dependency is not accepted, do not bypass it; implement only a separately approved non-dependent preparatory task and record the blocker.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: Minimum necessary personal data only; no identity document public/API leakage; reviewer actions audited; no self-approval; verification does not imply citizenship/residency beyond evidence; vote eligibility checks require the correct verification tier and constituency.

REQUIRED OUTPUT: working code; documented API and data changes; forward/rollback migrations where applicable; environment-variable template updates (never actual secrets); unit and integration tests; accessibility/security checks relevant to the phase; exact command/test results; updated `docs/phases/PH-05/phase-summary.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `AGENTS.md` and impacted architecture/domain/API/security/test docs. Do not mark ACCEPTED; E2E and phase-owner sign-off are required.

### Prompt C — Independent E2E Testing CodeGen

```text
You are an independent specialist code-generation agent working on NetaMonitor, an India-first, non-partisan public-information and civic-accountability web application. Its purpose is to let people inspect elected representatives, time-bounded offices/terms/party history, sourced promises and evidence, public projects and disclosures, then submit confidential verified-citizen evaluations. It is not an official election system, a political campaign tool, a popularity contest or a substitute for legal/electoral processes. The selected scope includes every feature in the phase roadmap: authentication; human and constituency verification; people/elections/terms; promises/evidence; voting; separate evidence and citizen measures; Admin and restricted SuperAdmin; corrections/replies/appeals; financial/business records; affiliations/NGOs/movements/protests/social accounts; schemes/projects; comparison; submissions/comments; gamification; notifications; English/Hindi/Marathi; maps/historical boundaries; imports; analytics/anti-manipulation; transparency/OpenAPI/open data; election-period safeguards; responsive PWA/offline mode.

FIRST ACTION: inspect the repository and read `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/PRODUCT_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `docs/SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/DOMAIN_MODEL.md`, `docs/SECURITY_MODEL.md`, `docs/DATA_DICTIONARY.md`, `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`, `docs/RBAC_MATRIX.md`, `docs/SCORING_METHODOLOGY.md`, `docs/VERIFICATION_POLICY.md`, `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY_REQUIREMENTS.md`, `docs/MODERATION_POLICY.md`, the relevant ADRs and this phase file. If a file does not yet exist, create or update it as required by the phase; do not invent undocumented decisions. Repository documentation is the source of truth, never a prior chat transcript. Do not ask the user to repeat requirements already recorded. If a requirement conflicts with an approved ADR or security policy, stop that specific change, explain the conflict in the handoff and propose an ADR rather than silently changing architecture.

PORTABILITY: use TypeScript, a React/Vite frontend, an API boundary (Hono/TypeScript is the initial adapter), PostgreSQL as the relational source of truth, SQL migrations, OpenAPI contracts and provider interfaces. Initial hosting may use Cloudflare Pages/Workers, Supabase PostgreSQL/Auth and Cloudflare R2, but domain logic must not depend on those vendors. Keep auth, object storage, email/push, maps/geocoding and external data sources behind replaceable adapters. No client-side secrets. Do not place privileged service-role keys in browser code. All authorisation and business rules are enforced server-side. Apply deny-by-default database/API permissions and least privilege.

TRUST AND PRIVACY NON-NEGOTIABLES: distinguish registered/contact-confirmed, identity-verified and constituency-verified. OTP is not proof of identity or residence. Do not scrape voter rolls or implement Aadhaar biometric/eKYC. Minimise identity data; encrypt temporary verification evidence; apply access limits and retention/deletion. Separate voter identity mapping from ballot choice; public APIs expose aggregates only and suppress small cohorts. No public individual vote history. Admins must not approve themselves. SuperAdmin is hidden from ordinary navigation but is not secured by obscurity; enforce server-side permissions, MFA, visible support impersonation and audit it. Impersonation cannot cast votes or make identity/verification decisions. Audit logs are append-only and tamper-evident with hash chaining and independent checkpoints; do not promise that a single database is absolutely immutable.

CONTENT AND SCORING: every material factual assertion needs source, attribution, date, status and correction path. Separate sourced/editorial assessment from citizen vote distributions. Do not turn association into proof of wrongdoing. Financial amounts must be dated, sourced and labelled self-declared/officially recorded/corroborated; never call them independently audited net worth without proof. A promise score includes only due, assessable, attributable commitments; default equal weighting and a public versioned formula; exclude not-due, unverifiable, withdrawn and out-of-scope commitments with reasons. Citizen measures remain separate and display sample sizes; do not treat votes as proof or official election results. No paid ranking, no paid vote weighting, no political advertising in the MVP.

ENGINEERING QUALITY: use semantic accessible HTML, keyboard support, WCAG 2.2 AA target, responsive mobile-first layouts, safe validation, output encoding, rate limiting, structured logs without secrets/PII, migrations and rollback, unit/integration tests, and deterministic synthetic fixtures. Use OWASP ASVS 5.0 as a security verification reference. Do not use real people's identity documents or real voter records in tests. All test identities and politicians must be clearly synthetic. Never commit credentials, personal data, generated production data or local secrets. Never weaken security to make a test pass.

DELIVERY DISCIPLINE: work only on the assigned phase and its accepted dependencies. Keep changes reviewable. Do not implement unrelated future phases. Update `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, the current phase file, architecture/data/API/security documents and ADRs whenever they change. Include exact file paths, migrations, env vars, tests and commands in the handoff. Do not claim completion unless every acceptance criterion is demonstrated. Do not deploy to production or incur paid services without explicit approval. Use free-tier assumptions only for a pilot and instrument usage/quotas.

PHASE ASSIGNMENT: PH-05 — Identity and constituency verification workflow
ROLE: You are Agent 3: INDEPENDENT E2E / QA CODEGEN. You are independent of the developer and must test the running feature against requirements, not merely inspect unit tests. Read the approved designs and acceptance criteria; create/use synthetic fixtures and separate test accounts for each role. Exercise real browser flows and API boundaries, permissions, validation, failure states, responsive viewports and privacy/security edge cases relevant to this phase. Do not change production code to hide defects. You may create test-only utilities/fixtures and isolated test configuration. Execute tests and save reports, screenshots/video/traces where useful, reproducible defect tickets with severity, steps, expected/actual results and evidence. Distinguish a blocked test from a passed test. Do not mark the phase accepted while critical/high defects or untested acceptance criteria remain. Do not access production personal data.

PHASE GOAL: Establish separate contact, identity and constituency-eligibility states while minimising and protecting personal data.

YOUR PHASE-SPECIFIC TEST MISSION: Test the three distinct states (registered, identity-verified, constituency-verified), reviewer permissions, self-approval prevention, document leakage, signed URL expiry, upload validation, rejection/appeal, retention deletion and inability to vote outside eligibility. Test non-digital/manual exception route.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA UNDER TEST: Minimum necessary personal data only; no identity document public/API leakage; reviewer actions audited; no self-approval; verification does not imply citizenship/residency beyond evidence; vote eligibility checks require the correct verification tier and constituency.

TEST EXECUTION: Read the design and developer handoff, then create a requirement-to-test traceability table. Use synthetic fixtures only and a clean test environment. Test all changed browser flows and API permission boundaries, relevant device sizes, error/empty/loading conditions, accessibility and privacy/security cases. Run the tests independently; do not infer a pass from developer reports. Save Playwright tests, test data setup/teardown, screenshots/traces where relevant, machine-readable result and human-readable report under `tests/e2e/PH-05/` and `docs/phases/PH-05/phase-summary.md`.

EXIT REPORT: Return PASS / FAIL / BLOCKED for each acceptance criterion, total pass/fail/blocked counts, exact commands, environment/build ID, defect severity and reproducible steps. Any critical/high defect, privacy leak, privilege bypass, inaccurate score, vote integrity failure or missing test blocks acceptance. Do not alter application code to hide a defect. Recommend ACCEPTED only when every required criterion is tested and passed.
```

---

# PH-06 — Geographic hierarchy, constituencies and election calendar

**Dependencies:** 01, 02  
**Goal:** Model state/district/local-body/ward/assembly/parliament constituencies and election events with effective dates and historical boundaries.

### Phase specification

**UI/design scope:** Design location selection, constituency search, hierarchy breadcrumbs, election calendar, historical boundary selection and ambiguous-address handling. Keep location optional for browsing and explain when it is used for eligibility.

**Development scope:** Create normalised geography, constituency, local-body, election-event and boundary-version schemas; importable identifiers; effective-from/to dates; parent/child relationships; timezone strategy; search API and admin CRUD. Prepare PostGIS-compatible geometry fields but keep map rendering for Phase 21.

**Independent E2E scope:** Test overlapping constituency types, boundary versions, renamed/reorganised districts, constituency selection ambiguity, effective date queries, invalid hierarchies, duplicate identifiers and safe handling of user-selected versus verified constituency.

**Acceptance gate:** Historical geography is versioned; elected terms can reference the correct boundary version; data supports India first but is not India-hardcoded; search and selection work on mobile and with assistive tech.

Execute tests and save reports, screenshots/video/traces where useful, reproducible defect tickets with severity, steps, expected/actual results and evidence. Distinguish a blocked test from a passed test. Do not mark the phase accepted while critical/high defects or untested acceptance criteria remain. Do not access production personal data.

PHASE GOAL: Model state/district/local-body/ward/assembly/parliament constituencies and election events with effective dates and historical boundaries.

YOUR PHASE-SPECIFIC TEST MISSION: Test overlapping constituency types, boundary versions, renamed/reorganised districts, constituency selection ambiguity, effective date queries, invalid hierarchies, duplicate identifiers and safe handling of user-selected versus verified constituency.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA UNDER TEST: Historical geography is versioned; elected terms can reference the correct boundary version; data supports India first but is not India-hardcoded; search and selection work on mobile and with assistive tech.

TEST EXECUTION: Read the design and developer handoff, then create a requirement-to-test traceability table. Use synthetic fixtures only and a clean test environment. Test all changed browser flows and API permission boundaries, relevant device sizes, error/empty/loading conditions, accessibility and privacy/security cases. Run the tests independently; do not infer a pass from developer reports. Save Playwright tests, test data setup/teardown, screenshots/traces where relevant, machine-readable result and human-readable report under `tests/e2e/PH-06/` and `docs/phases/PH-06/phase-summary.md`.

Any critical/high defect, privacy leak, privilege bypass, inaccurate score, vote integrity failure or missing test blocks acceptance. Do not alter application code to hide a defect. Recommend ACCEPTED only when every required criterion is tested and passed.
